, we will need to describe how to perform measurements corresponding to the projectors
V^{L}\cdot V^{L,\dagger}
and
V^{R}\cdot V^{R,\dagger}
. In fact, we will need to implement these measurements coherently, i.e., apply the unitary that computes the binary measurement outcome onto an external qubit. This can be done as follows (for simplicity, we only describe the procedure for implementing the coherent
V^{L}\cdot V^{L,\dagger}
measurement, as the coherent
V^{R}\cdot V^{R,\dagger}
measurement is symmetric).

Input:
a state
\ket*{\psi}=\sum_{x,L,R}\alpha_{x,L,R}\ket*{x}\ket*{L}\ket*{R}
.

1.

Add
m^{\prime}
ancillary qubits
\ket*{0^{m'}}
and then apply
U^{L,\dagger}
.

2.

Initialize a new one-qubit register to
\ket*{0}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}}
. Controlled on the last
m
qubits of the rest of the state (i.e., all registers except for
\mathsf{B}
) being
0^{m}
, apply a Pauli
X
to the
\mathsf{B}
register. By definition of
U^{L,\dagger}
, this Pauli
X
is applied if and only if the original input state was in the image of
V^{L}\cdot V^{L,\dagger}
.

3.

Apply
U^{L}
to the non-
\mathsf{B}
registers.

Now, recall the definition of
V
.

Definition 44
.

The symmetric path-recording oracle is the operator
V
defined as

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle=V^{L}\cdot(\mathsf{Id}-V^{R}\cdot V^{R,\dagger})+(\mathsf{Id}-V^{L}\cdot V^{L,\dagger})\cdot V^{R,\dagger}.

(A.8)

We sketch an implementation of a forward query to the symmetric path-recording oracle
V
. The inverse query is symmetric by swapping
L
and
R
.

Input:
a state
\ket*{\psi}=\sum_{x,L,R}\alpha_{x,L,R}\ket*{x}\ket*{L}\ket*{R}
.

1.

Add two ancilla qubits initialized at
\ket*{0}
to obtain
\ket*{0}\ket*{0}\ket*{\psi}
.

2.

Implement coherent measurement
V^{R}\cdot V^{R,\dagger}
, writing the outcome onto the first ancilla qubit.

3.

Apply the following controlled operation:

•

Controlled on the first ancilla qubit being
1
, apply
V^{L}
.

•

Controlled on the first ancilla qubit being
0
, apply
V^{R,\dagger}
. Then, apply the coherent measurement
V^{L}\cdot V^{L,\dagger}
, writing the outcome onto the second ancilla qubit.

4.

Measure the second ancilla qubit, and abort if the outcome is
1
.

5.

Apply the coherent measurement of
V^{L}\cdot V^{L,\dagger}
with the outcome applied onto the first ancilla qubit.

6.

Trace out the remaining ancilla qubit (the first one), which is guaranteed to be
\ket{0}
.

Appendix B
The path-recording framework

In this section, we develop a mathematical framework by generalizing the path-recording oracle introduced in
Section

4
.
This new framework enables one to develop modified versions of path-recording oracle in which the set of relations that the oracle uses is restricted to a subset
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}\subseteq\mathcal{R}^{\mathsf{inj}}
of the set of all injective relations.
This mathematical framework offers flexibility for establishing indistinguishability from Haar-random unitary via the path-recording oracle.

To develop the path-recording framework, we define the following notations.

•

t_{\mathrm{max}}
is an integer between
1
and
N
sets the maximum size of the relations. This integer also sets the limit on how many queries we can make to the path-recording oracle. In the canonical path-recording oracle introduced in
Section

4.1
,
t_{\mathrm{max}}
is equal to
N
.

•

\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}_{t}
is a subset of all the injective relations
\mathcal{R}^{\mathsf{inj}}_{t}
of size
t
for any
0\leq t\leq t_{\max}
. In particular, we require the subset for the maximum
t
to be non-empty:
|\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}_{t_{\mathrm{max}}}|\geq 1
.

•

\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}\coloneqq\cup_{t=0}^{t_{\mathrm{max}}}\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}_{t}
. The set
\mathcal{S}
restricts the relations that the path-recording oracle could use.

We define the following two constraints on the restricted set
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
.

Definition 45

(Consistency)
.

We say the set
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
of relations is consistent if

\displaystyle\forall(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\in[N]^{t},\quad\exists(y_{1},\ldots,y_{t})\in[N]^{t},

(B.1)

\displaystyle\,\,\text{such that}\quad\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{t}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}.

(B.2)

Furthermore, if
\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{t}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
, then for any
0\leq\tau\leq t
,
\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{\tau}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
.

The
consistency constraint
ensures that all possible
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\in[N]^{t}
are valid. This is central for path-recording oracle because the adversary algorithm can choose the inputs
x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}
arbitrarily.
The constraint also ensures that all relations in
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
are “meaningful” because they can all be obtained by adding in each tuple
(x_{i},y_{i})
one by one while maintaining in the restricted subset
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
.

Definition 46

(Uniform growth)
.

We say the set
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
of relations satisfies the uniform growth constraint if for all
0\leq t<t_{\max}
, there exists
\mathcal{Z}_{t}\geq 1
, such that for all
x\in[N]
and
R\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}_{t}
,

\mathcal{Z}_{t}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in[N],\,\mathrm{s.t.}\\
R\cup\{(x,y)\}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}_{t+1}\end{subarray}}1.

(B.3)

The
uniform growth constraint
ensures the number of
y
that can be used to grow the relation
R
by by size one is uniform across all
x\in[N]
and all relations
R
of the same size.
We illustrate these two constraints with the following examples.

•

\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
contains all relations where the first
k
bits in
y_{1},\ldots,y_{t}\in[N]
are distinct. In this case,
t_{\max}=2^{k}
. Furthermore,
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
is consistent and satisfies the uniform growth constraint.

•

\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
contains all relations
R
where
R=\{(x_{i},x_{i})\}_{i=1}^{|R|}
. In this case,
t_{\max}=N
. And
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
is consistent and satisfies the uniform growth constraint.

•

\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}=\{R\in\mathcal{R}^{\mathsf{inj}}\,\,|\,\,|R|=N\}
. In this case,
t_{\max}=N
. However,
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
is not consistent and does not satisfy the uniform growth constraint because it violates
\mathcal{Z}_{t}\geq 1
.

For any consistent set
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
of relations, we have
\varnothing\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
because we can take
\tau=0
in
Definition

45
for any relation
R\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
to obtain that
\varnothing\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
.

B.1
Defining
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
and the
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
state

We now define the behavior of the
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
-restricted path-recording oracle.

Definition 47

(
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
-restricted path-recording oracle)
.

Given any consistent set
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
of relations.
The
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
-restricted path-recording oracle
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
is a linear map

V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}):\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{R}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{R}}

(B.4)

defined as follows. For all
0\leq t<t_{\max}
,
R\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}_{t}
, and
x\in[N]
,

V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}):\ket*{x}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\mapsto\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{Z}_{x,R}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in[N],\\
R\cup\{(x,y)\}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}_{t+1}\end{subarray}}\ket*{y}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{R \cup\{(x,y)\}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}},

(B.5)

The normalization factor
\mathcal{Z}_{x,R}
is given by

\mathcal{Z}_{x,R}\coloneqq\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in[N],\\
R\cup\{(x,y)\}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}_{t+1}\end{subarray}}1\geq 1,

(B.6)

where the last inequality follows from the consistency constraint that for any
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\in[N]^{t}
, there exists
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{t})\in[N]^{t}
, such that
\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{t}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
.

Next, we define the
G
-rotated
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
state, which represents the global state after an adversary has queried the
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
-restricted path-recording oracle multiple times.

Definition 48

(
G
-rotated
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
state)
.

Given a consistent set
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
, an
n
-qubit unitary
G
and a
t
-query adversary
\mathcal{A}
with forward queries specified by a
t
-tuple of unitaries
(A_{1,{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}},\dots,A_{t,{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}})
, the
G
-rotated
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
state is

\ket*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj}) \cdot G}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\coloneqq\prod_{i=1}^{t}\Big(V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})\cdot G_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}}\cdot A_{i,{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}}\Big)\ket*{0}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}}\ket*{\varnothing}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}.

(B.7)

The
G
-rotated
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
state
\ket*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj}) \cdot G}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}
is the state of the entire system after the adversary has made
t
queries to
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})\cdot G
, and includes the adversary’s query register (
\mathsf{A}
), auxiliary register (
\mathsf{B}
), and the purifying registers (
\mathsf{R}
), after
t
queries to the oracle.

B.2
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
is a partial isometry

A crucial property of the
G
-rotated
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
state is that it maintains unit norm up to
t_{\max}
queries. We formalize this in the following lemma:

Lemma B.1

(
G
-rotated
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
state has unit norm)
.

For any consistent set
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
of relations, any adversary
\mathcal{A}
making
t\leq t_{\max}
forward queries to an
n
-qubit oracle, and any
n
-qubit unitary
G
, the
G
-rotated
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
state
\ket*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj}) \cdot G}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}
has unit norm.

To prove this lemma, we first need to establish that the
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
-restricted path-recording oracle
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
acts as a partial isometry on certain states. This is formalized in the following lemma:

Lemma B.2

(Partial Isometry)
.

For any consistent set
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
of relations, the
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
-restricted path-recording oracle
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
is an isometry on the subspace of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{R}}
spanned by the states
\ket*{x}\ket*{R}
for
x\in[N]
and
R\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
such that
\absolutevalue{R}<t_{\max}
.

Proof of Lemma
B.2
.

To prove that
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
is an isometry on the specified subspace, it suffices to show that for all
x,x^{\prime}\in[N]
and
R,R^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
with
\absolutevalue{R},\absolutevalue{R'}<t_{\max}
,

\bra*{x'}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}}\bra*{R'}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})^{\dagger}\cdot V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})\ket*{x}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}=\innerproduct*{x'}{x}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}}\cdot\innerproduct*{R'}{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}.

(B.8)

The proof proceeds in the same way as the proof of
Lemma

4.1
after one notes the fact that the normalization factor
\mathcal{Z}{x,R}\geq 1
from the consistency of the set
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
.
∎

We can now prove
Lemma

B.1
.

Proof of
Lemma

B.1
.

Note that
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
is consistent implies
\varnothing\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}_{0}
. Hence, we can use
Lemma

B.2
to establish this lemma via the same mathematical induction as the proof of
Lemma

4.2
.
∎

B.3
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
is right unitary invariant

So far, we have not used the uniform growth constraint.
To show that
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
is (exactly) right unitary invariant, we need to utilize the uniform growth constraint.

Lemma B.3

(Right unitary invariance)
.

Given a consistent set
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
of relations that satisfies the uniform growth constraint. For any
n
-qubit unitary
G
, we have

\ket*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj}) \cdot G}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}=(G_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X},1}^{(t)}}}\otimes\ldots\otimes G_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X},t}^{(t)}}})\ket*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}.

(B.9)

Lemma

B.3
implies right unitary invariance since

\displaystyle\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}(\outerproduct*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj}) \cdot G}_t}{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj}) \cdot G}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}})

\displaystyle=\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}((G_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X},1}^{(t)}}}\otimes\ldots\otimes G_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X},t}^{(t)}}})\outerproduct*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}(G_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X},1}^{(t)}}}\otimes\ldots\otimes G_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X},t}^{(t)}}})^{\dagger})

(by
Lemma

B.3
)

\displaystyle=\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}(\outerproduct*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}).

(by the cyclic property of
\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}
)

The first line corresponds to the adversary’s view after making
t
queries to
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})\cdot G_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}}
, while the last line corresponds to its view after making
t
queries to
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
.

Fact 13

(Explicit form of the
G
-rotated
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
state)
.

Given a consistent set
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
of relations that satisfies the uniform growth constraint. The definition of
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
and
\ket*{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}
enable us to expand the
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
state after
t
queries to obtain

\displaystyle\ket*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj}) \cdot G}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\mathcal{Z}_{i}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\in[N]^{t}\\
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{t})\in[N]^{t}_{\dist}\\
R=\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{t}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}_{t}\end{subarray}}\left[\,\prod_{i=1}^{t}\Big(\outerproduct*{y_i}{x_i}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}}\cdot G_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}}\cdot A_{i,{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}}\Big)\ket*{0}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}}\,\right]\otimes\ket*{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}

(B.10)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{\mathcal{Z}_{i}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\in[N]^{t}\\
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{t})\in[N]^{t}_{\dist}\\
R=\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{t}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}_{t}\end{subarray}}\left[\,\prod_{i=1}^{t}\Big(\outerproduct*{y_i}{x_i}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}}\cdot G_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}}\cdot A_{i,{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}}\Big)\ket*{0}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}}\,\right]

\displaystyle\otimes\frac{1}{\sqrt{t!}}\sum_{\pi\in\mathsf{Sym}_{t}}\left(R_{\pi}\ket*{x_1}_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X},1}^{(t)}}}\dots\ket*{x_t}_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X},t}^{(t)}}}\right)\otimes\left(R_{\pi}\ket*{y_1}_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{Y},1}^{(t)}}}\dots\ket*{y_t}_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{Y},t}^{(t)}}}\right).

(B.11)

Proof of
Lemma

B.3
.

By utilizing
Fact

13
instead of
Fact

3
, we can prove this lemma in the same way as the proof of
Lemma

4.3
.
∎

B.4
Relation between
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
and
V
state

Using the states
\ket*{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}
, we can define the following restricted subspace projector based on the restricted subspace
\Pi^{\mathsf{restrict},t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}
.

Definition 49

(Restricted subspace projector)
.

For
0\leq t\leq t_{\max}
, we define the size-
t
restricted subspace projector
\Pi^{\mathsf{restrict},t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\Pi^{\mathsf{restrict},t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{R\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}_{t}}\outerproduct*{R}{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}.

(B.12)

The restricted subspace projector is defined as:

\Pi^{\mathsf{restrict}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\coloneqq\sum_{t=0}^{t_{\max}}\Pi^{\mathsf{restrict},t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}.

(B.13)

From
Fact

13
and the projector defined above, we immediately obtain the following equation relating the
G
-rotated
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
and the
\ket*{\Adv^{\pr\cdot G}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}
s.

Fact 14

(Relation between
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
and
V
state)
.

Given a consistent set
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
of relations that satisfies the uniform growth constraint.
For any
n
-qubit unitary
G
, we have

\sqrt{\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathcal{Z}_{i}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{(N-t)!}{N!}}\cdot\ket*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj}) \cdot G}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}=\Pi^{\mathsf{restrict}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\ket*{\Adv^{\pr\cdot G}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}},

(B.14)

where the prefactor
\sqrt{\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathcal{Z}_{i}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{(N-t)!}{N!}}
is between
0
and
1
because the
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
state
\ket*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj}) \cdot G}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}
has unit norm and the projected
V
state
\Pi^{\mathsf{restrict}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\ket*{\Adv^{\pr\cdot G}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}
has norm at most one.

B.5
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
is indistinguishable from Haar random unitaries

When the restricted set
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
of relations has a large enough growth, then
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
is indistinguishable from Haar-random unitaries.
This is formally captured by the following theorem.

Theorem 9

(
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}})
is indistinguishable from Haar random)
.

Given a consistent set
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}
of relations that satisfies the uniform growth constraint.
Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
t
-query oracle adversary with forward queries. Then

\displaystyle\mathsf{TD}\left(\E_{\mathcal{O}\leftarrow\mu_{\mathsf{Haar}}}\outerproduct*{\Adv_t^{\calO}}{\Adv_t^{\calO}},\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}\left(\outerproduct*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\right)\right)

(B.15)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2t(t-1)}{N+1}+2\left(1-\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathcal{Z}_{i}\cdot\frac{(N-t)!}{N!}\right).

(B.16)

Proof.

Using
Theorem

5
and triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{TD}\left(\E_{\mathcal{O}\leftarrow\mu_{\mathsf{Haar}}}\outerproduct*{\Adv_t^{\calO}}{\Adv_t^{\calO}},\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}\left(\outerproduct*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\right)\right)

(B.17)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2t(t-1)}{N+1}+\mathsf{TD}\left(\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}\left(\outerproduct*{\Adv^{\pr}_t}{\Adv^{\pr}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\right),\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}\left(\outerproduct*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\right)\right).

(B.18)

We can bound the second term as follows,

\displaystyle\mathsf{TD}\left(\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}\left(\outerproduct*{\Adv^{\pr}_t}{\Adv^{\pr}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\right),\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}\left(\outerproduct*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\right)\right)

(B.19)

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{TD}\left(\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}\left(\outerproduct*{\Adv^{\pr}_t}{\Adv^{\pr}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\right),\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}\left(\Pi^{\mathsf{restrict}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\outerproduct*{\Adv^{\pr\cdot G}_t}{\Adv^{\pr\cdot G}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\Pi^{\mathsf{restrict}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\right)\right)

(B.20)

\displaystyle+\mathsf{TD}\left(\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}\left(\Pi^{\mathsf{restrict}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\outerproduct*{\Adv^{\pr\cdot G}_t}{\Adv^{\pr\cdot G}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\Pi^{\mathsf{restrict}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\right),\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}\left(\outerproduct*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\right)\right).

(B.21)

Using
Lemma

2.2
and
Fact

14
,
Eq.

B.20
is equal to

\displaystyle 1-\bra*{\Adv^{\pr\cdot G}_t}\Pi^{\mathsf{restrict}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\ket*{\Adv^{\pr\cdot G}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}=\left(1-\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathcal{Z}_{i}\cdot\frac{(N-t)!}{N!}\right).

(B.22)

Again, using
Fact

14
,
Eq.

B.21
is equal to

\displaystyle\mathsf{TD}\left(\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathcal{Z}_{i}\cdot\frac{(N-t)!}{N!}\cdot\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}\left(\outerproduct*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\right),\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}\left(\outerproduct*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj})}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}}\right)\right)

(B.23)

\displaystyle=\left(1-\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathcal{Z}_{i}\cdot\frac{(N-t)!}{N!}\right).

(B.24)

Together, we obtained the stated result.
∎

Appendix C
An elementary proof of the gluing lemma

In this section, we show how to use the path-recording framework to establish an elementary proof of the gluing lemma recently shown in
[
30
]
. The proof in
[
30
]
makes use of representation theory and Weingarten calculus. Here, we present an elementary proof using the path-recording framework for analyzing Haar-random unitaries.

The gluing lemma presented in
[
30
]
shows that the composition of two Haar-random unitaries on system
\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}
and
\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}
that overlap only on a small number of qubits is indistinguishable from a Haar-random unitary on the entire system
\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}
.

Theorem 10

(Gluing two Haar-random unitaries)
.

Consider three systems
\mathsf{A}_{1},\mathsf{A}_{2},\mathsf{A}_{3}
of qubits with
\mathsf{A}=\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}
. Let
|\mathsf{A}_{1}|,|\mathsf{A}_{2}|,|\mathsf{A}_{3}|
denote the number of qubits in each system. Let
U_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}},U_{\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}},U_{\mathsf{A}}
be Haar-random unitaries on system
\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2},\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3},\mathsf{A}
, respectively. We have

\displaystyle\mathsf{TD}\left(\E_{U_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}},U_{\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}}}\outerproduct*{\Adv_t^{U_{\sA_1 \sA_2} U_{\sA_2 \sA_3}}}{\Adv_t^{U_{\sA_1 \sA_2} U_{\sA_2 \sA_3}}},\E_{U_{\mathsf{A}}}\outerproduct*{\Adv_t^{U_{\sA}}}{\Adv_t^{U_{\sA}}}\right)\leq\frac{9t(t-1)}{2^{|\mathsf{A}_{2}|}}.

(C.1)

Proof.

We approximate the three Haar-random unitaries
U_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}},U_{\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}},U_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}}
by three restricted sets
\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}}_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}},\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}(\mathsf{D})}_{\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}},\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}\mathsf{(CD)}}_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}}
of relations. The three subsets of injective relations are given as follows.

•

\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}(\mathsf{A}_{2})}_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}}
: Injective relations
R
over system
\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}
such that the system
\mathsf{A}_{2}
part of elements in the image
\imaginary(R)
are distinct, i.e., given
\imaginary(R)=\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{|R|}\}
,
y_{i,\mathsf{A}_{2}}\neq y_{j,\mathsf{A}_{2}}
for all
i\neq j
.

•

\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}(\mathsf{A}_{2})}_{\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}}
: Injective relations
R
over system
\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}
such that the system
\mathsf{A}_{2}
part of elements in the image
\imaginary(R)
are distinct.

•

\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}(\mathsf{A}_{2})}_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}}
: Injective relations
R
over system
\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}
such that the system
\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}
part of elements in the image
\imaginary(R)
are distinct.

Here, given a bitstring
y
, we denote
y_{\mathsf{A}_{2}}
to be the substring corresponding to bits in
\mathsf{A}_{2}
.
It is not hard to see that these restricted sets is consistent and satisfies the uniform growth constraint.
We consider the path recording oracle
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}(\mathsf{A}_{2})}_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}})
to act on system
\mathsf{A}_{1},\mathsf{A}_{2},\mathsf{R}_{1}
, the oracle
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}(\mathsf{A}_{2})}_{\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}})
to act on system
\mathsf{A}_{2},\mathsf{A}_{3},\mathsf{R}_{2}
, and the oracle
V(\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}(\mathsf{A}_{2})}_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}})
to act on system
\mathsf{A}_{1},\mathsf{A}_{2},\mathsf{A}_{3},\mathsf{R}_{3}
.
Let

\displaystyle\rho_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneq\E_{U_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}},U_{\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}}}\outerproduct*{\Adv_t^{U_{\sA_1 \sA_2} U_{\sA_2 \sA_3}}}{\Adv_t^{U_{\sA_1 \sA_2} U_{\sA_2 \sA_3}}},

(C.2)

\displaystyle\rho_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneq\E_{U_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}}}\Tr_{\mathsf{R}_{1}}\outerproduct*{\Adv_t^{U_{\sA_1 \sA_2} \pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj(\mathsf{\sA_2})}_{\sA_2 \sA_3})}}{\Adv_t^{U_{\sA_1 \sA_2} \pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj(\mathsf{\sA_2})}_{\sA_2 \sA_3})}},

(C.3)

\displaystyle\rho_{3}

\displaystyle\coloneq\Tr_{\mathsf{R}_{1}}\Tr_{\mathsf{R}_{2}}\outerproduct*{\Adv_t^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj}_{\sA_1\sA_2}) \pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj(\sA_2)}_{\sA_2 \sA_3})}}{\Adv_t^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj}_{\sA_1\sA_2}) \pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj(\sA_2)}_{\sA_2 \sA_3})}},

(C.4)

\displaystyle\rho_{4}

\displaystyle\coloneq\Tr_{\mathsf{R}_{3}}\outerproduct*{\Adv_t^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj(\sA_2)}_{\sA_1 \sA_2 \sA_3})}}{\Adv_t^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj(\sA_2)}_{\sA_1 \sA_2 \sA_3})}},

(C.5)

\displaystyle\rho_{5}

\displaystyle\coloneq\E_{U_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}}}\outerproduct*{\Adv_t^{U_{\sA_1 \sA_2 \sA_3}}}{\Adv_t^{U_{\sA_1 \sA_2 \sA_3}}}.

(C.6)

Using
Theorem

9
and properly computing the normalization factor
\mathcal{Z}_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{TD}(\rho_{1},\rho_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2t(t-1)}{2^{|\mathsf{A}_{2}|+|\mathsf{A}_{3}|}}+2\left(1-\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}(2^{|\mathsf{A}_{2}|+|\mathsf{A}_{3}|}-i2^{|\mathsf{A}_{3}|})\cdot\frac{(2^{|\mathsf{A}_{2}|+|\mathsf{A}_{3}|}-t)!}{(2^{|\mathsf{A}_{2}|+|\mathsf{A}_{3}|})!}\right),

(C.7)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2t(t-1)}{2^{|\mathsf{A}_{2}|+|\mathsf{A}_{3}|}}+2\left(1-\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}\left(1-i2^{-|\mathsf{A}_{2}|}\right)\right)\leq\frac{2t(t-1)}{2^{|\mathsf{A}_{2}|+|\mathsf{A}_{3}|}}+\frac{t(t-1)}{2^{|\mathsf{A}_{2}|}}\leq\frac{3t(t-1)}{2^{|\mathsf{A}_{2}|}}.

(C.8)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{TD}(\rho_{2},\rho_{3})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2t(t-1)}{2^{|\mathsf{A}_{1}|+|\mathsf{A}_{2}|}}+2\left(1-\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}(2^{|\mathsf{A}_{1}|+|\mathsf{A}_{2}|}-i2^{|A_{1}|})\cdot\frac{(2^{|\mathsf{A}_{1}|+|\mathsf{A}_{2}|}-t)!}{(2^{|\mathsf{A}_{1}|+|\mathsf{A}_{2}|})!}\right)\leq\frac{3t(t-1)}{2^{|\mathsf{A}_{2}|}},

(C.9)

\displaystyle\mathsf{TD}(\rho_{4},\rho_{5})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2t(t-1)}{2^{|\mathsf{A}|}}+2\left(1-\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}(2^{|\mathsf{A}|}-i2^{|\mathsf{A}_{1}|+|\mathsf{A}_{3}|})\cdot\frac{(2^{|\mathsf{A}|}-t)!}{(2^{|\mathsf{A}|})!}\right)\leq\frac{3t(t-1)}{2^{|\mathsf{A}_{2}|}}.

(C.10)

Finally, we show that
\rho_{3}=\rho_{4}
. Let
x||y
denote bitstring concatenation. Using the definition of the restricted subsets of injective relations, the explicit form of the purified state in
Fact

13
yields

\displaystyle\ket*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj(\sA_2)}_{\sA_1\sA_2}) \pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj(\sA_2)}_{\sA_2 \sA_3})}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}2}}}=\sqrt{\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{2^{|\mathsf{A}_{2}|+|\mathsf{A}_{3}|}-i2^{|\mathsf{A}_{3}|}}}\cdot\sqrt{\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{2^{|\mathsf{A}_{1}|+|\mathsf{A}_{2}|}-i2^{|\mathsf{A}_{1}|}}}\cdot

(C.11)

\displaystyle\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\in[2^{|A|}]^{t}\\
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{t})\in[2^{|A|}]^{t}_{\dist}\\
(z_{1},\ldots,z_{t})\in[2^{|A_{2}|}]^{t}_{\dist}\\
\mathrm{s.t.}\,\,(y_{1,A_{2}},\ldots,y_{t,A_{2}})\in[2^{|A_{2}|}]^{t}_{\dist}\\
R=\{(x_{i,A_{2}}||x_{i,A_{3}},z_{i,A_{2}}||y_{i,A_{3}})\}_{i=1}^{t}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}(\mathsf{A}_{2})}_{\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}}\\
S=\{(x_{i,A_{1}}||z_{i,A_{2}},y_{i,A_{1}}||y_{i,A_{2}})\}_{i=1}^{t}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}(\mathsf{A}_{2})}_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}}\end{subarray}}\left[\,\prod_{i=1}^{t}\Big(\outerproduct*{y_i}{x_i}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}}\cdot A_{i,{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}}\Big)\ket*{0}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}}\,\right]\otimes\ket*{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}1}}}\otimes\ket*{S}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}2}}},

(C.12)

and, similarly,
Fact

13
also yields

\displaystyle\ket*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj(\sA_2)}_{\sA_1\sA_2\sA_3})}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}3}}}=\sqrt{\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{2^{|\mathsf{A}|}-i2^{|\mathsf{A}_{1}|+|\mathsf{A}_{3}|}}}\cdot

(C.13)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\in[2^{|A|}]^{t}\\
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{t})\in[2^{|A|}]^{t}_{\dist}\\
\mathrm{s.t.}\,\,(y_{1,A_{2}},\ldots,y_{t,A_{2}})\in[2^{|A_{2}|}]^{t}_{\dist}\\
T=\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{t}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{inj}(\mathsf{A}_{2})}_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}}\end{subarray}}\left[\,\prod_{i=1}^{t}\Big(\outerproduct*{y_i}{x_i}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}}\cdot A_{i,{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}}\Big)\ket*{0}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}}\,\right]\otimes\ket*{T}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}3}}}.

(C.14)

We define a linear map
\mathsf{Uncompress}
that maps registers
\mathsf{R}_{3}
to registers
\mathsf{R}_{1},\mathsf{R}_{2}
. For any
T=\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{t}
such that
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{t})\in[2^{|A|}]^{t}
,
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{t})\in[2^{|A|}]^{t}_{\dist}
, and
(y_{1,A_{2}},\ldots,y_{t,A_{2}})\in[2^{|A_{2}|}]^{t}_{\dist}
, the linear map
\mathsf{Uncompress}
acts as

\mathsf{Uncompress}\ket*{T}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}3}}}\coloneq\sqrt{\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{2^{|\mathsf{A}_{2}|}-i}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(z_{1},\ldots,z_{t})\in[2^{|A_{2}|}]^{t}_{\dist}\\
\mathrm{s.t.}\,\,R=\{(x_{i,A_{2}}||x_{i,A_{3}},z_{i,A_{2}}||y_{i,A_{3}})\}_{i=1}^{t}\\
S=\{(x_{i,A_{1}}||z_{i,A_{2}},y_{i,A_{1}}||y_{i,A_{2}})\}_{i=1}^{t}\end{subarray}}\ket*{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}1}}}\ket*{S}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}2}}}.

(C.15)

One can directly check that
\mathsf{Uncompress}
is a partial isometry and
\ket*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj(\sA_2)}_{\sA_1\sA_2\sA_3})}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}3}}}
is in the domain of
\mathsf{Uncompress}
by construction. Furthermore, by definition, we have

\mathsf{Uncompress}\ket*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj(\sA_2)}_{\sA_1\sA_2\sA_3})}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}3}}}=\ket*{\Adv^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj(\sA_2)}_{\sA_1\sA_2}) \pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj(\sA_2)}_{\sA_2 \sA_3})}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}\mathsf{R}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}2}}}.

(C.16)

Because
\mathsf{Uncompress}
acts isometric in its domain, we have

\displaystyle\rho_{3}

\displaystyle=\Tr_{\mathsf{R}_{1}}\Tr_{\mathsf{R}_{2}}\outerproduct*{\Adv_t^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj}_{\sA_1\sA_2}) \pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj(\sA_2)}_{\sA_2 \sA_3})}}{\Adv_t^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj}_{\sA_1\sA_2}) \pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj(\sA_2)}_{\sA_2 \sA_3})}}

(C.17)

\displaystyle=\Tr_{\mathsf{R}_{3}}\outerproduct*{\Adv_t^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj(\sA_2)}_{\sA_1 \sA_2 \sA_3})}}{\Adv_t^{\pr(\mathcal{S}^{\inj(\sA_2)}_{\sA_1 \sA_2 \sA_3})}}=\rho_{4}.

(C.18)

Together, by a series of triangle inequality, we have

\mathsf{TD}(\rho_{1},\rho_{5})\leq\mathsf{TD}(\rho_{1},\rho_{2})+\mathsf{TD}(\rho_{2},\rho_{3})+\mathsf{TD}(\rho_{4},\rho_{5})\leq\frac{9t(t-1)}{2^{|\mathsf{A}_{2}|}},

(C.19)

which concludes the proof of this theorem by noting the definition of
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{5}
.
∎

As shown in
[
30
]
, one can iteratively apply the gluing lemma to glue many small Haar-random unitaries over small number of qubits into a pseudorandom unitary on the entire system. If we have an
n
-qubit system
\mathsf{A}
that is separated into consecutive subsystems
\mathsf{A}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{A}_{2m}
, we can glue together small Haar-random unitaries
U_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\mathsf{A}_{2}},U_{\mathsf{A}_{2}\mathsf{A}_{3}},\ldots
as follows,

U_{\mathsf{glued}}\coloneq\prod_{k=0}^{m-1}(U_{\mathsf{A}_{2+2k}\mathsf{A}_{3+2k}})\prod_{k=0}^{m-1}(U_{\mathsf{A}_{1+2k}\mathsf{A}_{2+2k}}).

(C.20)

Using triangle inequality, the trace distance between a
t
-query adversary output state that queries
U_{\mathsf{glued}}
versus Haar-random unitary is upper bounded by

2m\cdot\frac{9t(t-1)}{2^{\min_{i\in[2m]}|\mathsf{A}_{i}|}}.

(C.21)

If each subsystem is of size
\omega(\log n)
, then the glued unitary
U_{\mathsf{glued}}
will be a pseudorandom unitary secure against
\poly(n)
-time adversary. This can be seen by noting that a
\poly(n)
-time adversary can only make
t=\poly(n)
queries, so the trace distance between the adversary state querying
U_{\mathsf{glued}}
and Haar-random unitary is upper bounded by

2m\cdot\frac{9t(t-1)}{2^{\min_{i\in[2m]}|\mathsf{A}_{i}|}}\leq\frac{\poly(n)}{2^{\omega(\log n)}}=\mathrm{negl}(n).

(C.22)

By replacing the small Haar-random unitaries over
\omega(\log n)
qubits with small pseudorandom unitaries secure against subexponential adversary, one can show that the glued unitary
U
is an
n
-qubit pseudorandom unitary secure against
\poly(n)
-time adversary.

Assuming the subexponential hardness of LWE
[
29
]
, we have proved that a pseudorandom unitary secure against subexponential adversary can be generated in polynomial-depth on any circuit geometry using the
PFC
construction, including a 1D geometry.
Hence, an
n
-qubit pseudorandom unitary secure against polynomial adversary can be generated in
\poly\log(n)
depth on any circuit geometry.
This work fills in the gap in
[
30
]
that assumes the
PFC
construction forms a pseudorandom unitary under LWE hardness to rigorously establish the surprising fact that pseudorandom unitaries can be generated in extremely low depth under standard cryptographic assumptions.
